                                        WARNING

                                           HIGH VOLTAGE

                                                Is used in this equipment

                                      DEATH ON CONTACT
                         MAY RESULT IF SAFETY PRECAUTIONS

                                      ARE NOT OBSERVED

 Maintenance adjustments of this equipment are made with power applied.Be careful
 when working near the interior of the equipment, or near the ac power distribution.

                                        WARNING

                                  VENTILATION IS ESSENTIAL

 To prevent asphyxiation, ventilate the AN/TRC-138 at all times when it is occupied.

                                      DON’T TAKE CHANCES!

 Operator and maintenance personnel should be familiar with the requirements of the TB
 SIG291 before attempting installation or operation of the equipment covered in this
 manual. Failure to follow requirements of TB SIG 291 could result in injury or DEATH.
 Prior to erecting the antenna, read the WARNING and SAFETY pages in chapter 2.

When                                WARNING                              in the actual
                AVOID INJURY TO PERSONNEL

      lifting or loading assemblages on a truck, only personnel engaged

loading operatica should be permitted near the truck, lifting device, and assemblage.

When installing the antenna masts, do not release the handbrake when the elevator plat-

form is not flush with the mast section (para 2-4).

Make sure the assemblage is properly grounded before the input power is connected (para

2-3).
If a Radio Set AN/VRC-46 is used in the assemblage, dangerous RF voltages exist on

the AS-1729 antenna components while transmitting. Prior to installation of the

AS-1729/VRC whip antenna, survey the area carefully for location of power lines, their

height above the ground level and their proximity to the installation site. The

AS-1729/VRC must be installed as far away as possible from Electric Power lines, but
never closer than twice its height. Failure to follow these requirements could result in in-

jury or death.

         WARNING

TELESCOPING ANTENNA  TYPICAL TOWER          EXTENDED RANGE   DOUBLET ANTENNA

MAST                                        ANTENNA

NEVER ERECT THESE LONG RANGE ANTENNAS DIRECTLY UNDER POWER LINES.

IF YOU MUST ERECT THESE LONG RANGE   ANTENNAS NEAR POWERLINES, POWERLINE POLES OR
 TOWERS, OR BUILDINGS WITH OVERHEAD  POWERLINE CONNECTIONS, NEVER PUT THE ANTENNA
                                     HEIGHT FROM. THE BASE OF THE POWERLINE, POLE,
CLOSER THAN TWO TIMES THE ANTENNA
 TOWER OR BUILDINGS,

NEVER ATTEMPT TO ERECT ANY LONG RANGE ANTENNA WITHOUT A FULL TEAM.

BEFORE ERECTINGANY LONG RANGE ANTENNA, INSPECT ALL THE PARTS MAKING UP THE
ANTENNA KIT. DO NOT ERECT THE ANTENNA IF ANY PARTS ARE MISSING OR DAMAGED,

:DO AS MUCH OF THE ASSEMBLY WORK AS POSSIBLE ON THE GROUND.

WHEN ERECTING THE ANTENNA, ALLOW ONLY TEAM PERSONNEL IN THE ERECTION AREA,

  MAKE SURE THAT THE AREA FOR THE ANCHORS  IS FIRM, IF THE GROUND IS-MARSHYOR SANDY,
GET SPECIFIC INSTRUCTIONS FROM YOUR CREW   CHIEF OR SUPERVISOR ON HOW TO REINFORCE

 THE ANCHORS,

_WHEN SELECTING LOCATIONS FOR ANCHORS, AVOID TRAVELED AREAS AND ROADS, IF YOU CANNOT
_AVOID THESE AREAS, GET SPECIFIC INSTRUCTIONS FROM YOUR SUPERVISOR AS TO WHAT CLEAR—
ANCE YOUR GUY WIRES AND ROPES MUST HAVE OVER THE TRAVELED AREAS AND ROAD.

CLEARLY MARK ALL GUY WIRES AND ROPES WITH THE WARNING FLAGS OR SIGNS SUPPLIED BY YOUR
| UNIT. INAN EMERGENCY, USE STRIPS OF WHITE CLOTH AS WARNING STREAMERS.

IF YOU SUSPECT THAT POWERLINES HAVE MADE ACCIDENTAL CONTACT WITH YOUR ANTENNA,        STOP
OPERATING, ROPE OFF THE ANTENNA AREA, AND NOTIFY YOUR SUPERIORS,

 IF THE WEATHER IN YOUR AREA CAN CAUSE ICE TO FORM ON YOUR LONG RANGE ANTENNA AND ITS
 GUY WIRES AND ROPES, ADD EXTRA GUYS TO SUPPORT THE SYSTEM, ROPE OFF THE AREA AND
| POST IT WITH WARNING SIGNS LIKE “ BEWARE OF FALLING ICE:

DO NOT TRY TO ERECT ANY ANTENNA DURING AN ELECTRICAL STORM.

KEEP A SHARP EYE ON YOUR ANCHORS AND GUYS.  CHECK THEM DAILY AND IMMEDIATELY
BEFORE AND AFTER BAD WEATHER,

                                                                  a. Operate LIGHT 1 switch to ON; roadside bank of fluorescent ceiling
                                                                      lights on each strip light.
                                                                  b. Operate LIGHT 2 switch to ON; curbside bank of fluorescent ceiling
                                                                      lights on each strip light.
                                                                  c. Operate INCANDESCENT COLD START LIGHTS switch to ON;
                                                                      incandescent ceiling lights light.
                                                                  d. Check POWER DISTRIBUTION BOX; AMPERES AC meter will in-
                                                                      dicate slight drain (not exceeding 2 amperes).
                                                                  Close door, but leave vents open; inspect for light tightness.
                                                                  Operate BYPASS-BLACKOUT switch to OFF and open door; assem-
                                                                     blage lights extinguish.
                                                                  Operate BYPASS-BLACKOUT switch to ON with door open; assemblage
                                                                     lights light.
                                                                  Operate BLOWER-1 switch to ON; exhaust blower No. 2 will operate.

                                                                  a. Operate HEAT-OFF-FAN switch to HEAT, operate TEM-
                                                                      PERATURE control and note that warm air blows from front of
                                                                       heater.
                                                                  b. Operate HEAT-OFF-FAN switch to FAN; fan blows air and heating
                                                                      element ceases to glow.
                                                                  c. Operate HEAT-OFF-FAN switch to OFF.

                                                                  a. Check that T-1054/GRC-144 and R-1467/GRC-144 PUSH-TO-
                                                                      TURN knobs and thumbwheels are at required frequencies.

                                                                      to ON.

                                                                      for 2 seconds or activate in accordance with called terminal or repeater
                                                                       identification code; RING indicator will light amber.
                                                                  d. When distant operator answers, converse by operating push-to-talk
                                                                      switch on H-156/U and check T-1054/GRC-144 SPEAKER
                                                                      VOLUME control by adjusting and listening for volume change.

                                                                      H-156/U in cradle.

X-MODE receptacle                                Provides manual tuning of radio in 0.05-MHz steps (00, 05, 10, 15, etc).

                                                 Controls de power to radio:

                                                    Position                            Function

                                                 OFF BREAKER-                 Turns off dc power to radio; and resets

                                                 RESET                        circuit breakers when they are tripped.

                                                 LOW                          Turns on dc power to radio; when radio is

                                                                              keyed for transmission, it transmits on

                                                                              low rf power (0.5 to 8 watts).

                                                 HIGH                         Turns on dc power to radio; when radio is

                                                                              keyed for transmission, it transmits on

                                                                                                 high rf power (35 watts, min).
                                                 For reception, selects type of squelch function to be used; during

                                                 transmission, 150-Hz squelch tone is transmitted in all positions of
                                                 the switch except OLD ON.

                                                    Position                            Function

                                                 NEW ON and OLD               Turns off (squelches receiver to preven

                                                 ON                           rushing noise from being heard unti

                                                                              squelch signal is received.

                                                                              a. In NEW ON, 150-Hz squelch tone,

                                                                              unsquelches the receiver.

                                                                              b. In OLD ON, noise that is part of the

                                                                              distant transmitter carrier frequency

                                                                              unsquelches the receiver.

                                                 NEW OFF and OLD              Receiver is in unsquelched condition (on
                                                    OFF                         standby, rushing noise is heard).

                                                 When to right, it confines the SQUELCH switch to two upper positions
                                                    (NEW) or two lower positions (OLD).

                                                 Controls audio output.
                                                 Connections for following functions:

                                                    a. Audio accessory (such as microphone and headset) for radio
                                                        transmission and reception.

                                                    b. Retransmission cable when radio is set up for radio relay operation.
                                                    c. Radio/wire (R/W) integration cable for radio transmission and

                                                        reception to be controlled by remote control equipment situated at
                                                        a site remote from the radio (such as AN/GSA~7).
                                                 Connection for following functions:
                                                    a. Audio accessory (such as microphone or headset) for radio trans
                                                         mission and reception).
                                                    b. Loudspeaker for radio reception.

                                                 Connection for control cable, CX-4722/VRC, to antenna matching unit.

                                                    Control of antenna matching unit networks is automatic and occur
                                                   when MC control and BAND switch are positioned.

                                                 Connection for capie to X-mode equipment (such as telephone multiplex,
                                                    secure voice, and digital data equipment) for control of radio trans-
                                                   mission and reception by X-mode equipment. The X-MODE-
                                                   NORMAL switch inside the radio must also be set to X-MODE

                                                   position when X-mode operation is used.
